import numpy as np
import time

def _default_length():
    try:
        seq = height_sequence_1
    except NameError:
        return 2535
    if isinstance(seq, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)):
        n = len(seq)
        if 1 <= n <= 50000:
            return int(n)
    return 2535

def _uniform_list(n):
    return [1.0] * int(n)

def _safe_score(seq):
    try:
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
    return val if val == val and val != float('inf') else float('inf')

```
def _perturb_uniform(n, rng):
    base = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    # choose few positions, add small relative perturbed values
    m = max(1, int(n * rng.random(1, 0.05)))
    idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
    base[idx] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1e-6, size=m))
    # maybe force nonnegative
    base = np.clip(base, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return base

def propose_candidate(...):
    n = _default_length()
    best_seq = _uniform_list(n)
    best_score = _safe_score(best_seq)
    print(...)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s)-1.0)
    # To keep exact optimum, only accept strictly lower scores.
    # Limit perturbations to tiny relative changes; if no lower, uniform.
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       cand = _perturb_uniform(n, rng).tolist()
       # Optional use a very small n=2 numerical candidate early
       score = _safe_score(cand)
       if score < best_score:
           best_seq = cand; best_score=score; print
       # if n large, few iterations; if budget >, can continue? But no improvement likely. To avoid wasting, break after 200? 
```

1. **Active set LP (SOTA)**:

But direct may be enough. For many iterations, FFT faster. Implement top-level `convolve_full_x` using `np.fft.rft`? We can write our own convolution for nonnegative real:
```
def full_convolution(a):
    n = a.size
    size = 1
    while size < 2*n -1: size <<=1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    fa *= fa
    return np.fft.irfft(fa, size)[:2*n-1]
```
This is O(LlogL), for n=2535 size8192, very fast. Floating errors small. For max maybe okay. For evaluator final use exact np.convolve. For search use FFT.

We need nonnegative f on [0,1] minimizing peak of f*f. This is a classical problem: minimize the supremum of the autocorrelation of a probability measure. By Delsarte? If allow signed, flat. Convolution has atoms at sums 2x_l, x_l+x_m. Peak max pair weight product (twice cross for distinct). Need distribute weights and positions to balance max pair sum. This is like arranging weighted points so all pairwise sums have small max weight. For M atoms, number of pair sums M(M+1)/2. Nonnegative measures minimizing max convolution mass: This relates to "provably prime x"? Maybe the optimal is a **dyadic** distribution (Bernoulli convolution) that makes pair sums balanced. For binary choice at each scale with probabilities, convolution distribution is the sum of independent Bernoulli variables. The maximum point mass of the sum distribution? Detailed gradient descent:

Given a sum1 length n.
```
def smooth_weights(c, beta):
    m = c.max()
    z = beta*(c-m)
    # clip z < -745 to avoid underflow, but exp(-inf)=0
    z = np.clip(z, -745, 700)
    p = np.exp(z)
    s = p.sum()
    if s ==0 or not finite: return np.full_like(c, 1.0/len(c))
    return p/s
```
Gradient from weights:
```
def grad_from_weights(w, a, n):
    wr = w[::-1]
    # use np.convolve? For gradient direct O(n*len(w)) maybe okay for small; use FFT.
    # To avoid complex roundoff, use np.convolve for n<=512? direct C maybe okay.
```
We already have convolution function. Could use `np.convolve(wr, a, mode='full')` direct. For n=2535, wr length5069, a2535, direct ~12.8M, maybe 0.01s. Good. Use direct for simplicity and accuracy. For many iterations (10000) 128B ops too high. Use FFT maybe. But we can limit iterations. Let's implement FFT convolution forboth convolution and gradient to be fast.

Top-level FFT conv helper:
```
def _fft_full(a, b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    n=len(a); m=len(b); need=n+m-1
    size=1
    while size<need: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,size); fa*=fb
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa,size)[:need]
    # tiny negative due to roundoff: max(0) maybe? For c nonnegative, clip small negatives.
    return out
```
But for objective max, tiny negative irrelevant. For gradient, w and a positive, roundoff can give tiny negative; clip 0.

Compute weights with c from `_fft_full(a)`. `max_c = c.max`. If max_c<=0, return uniform? But sum1 positive.

Score custom: `2*n*max_c` for sum1. Since actual evaluator with values scaled arbitrary same. If we want compare with evaluator for final, evaluate once.

Update:
```
def multiplicative_step(a, beta, eta, momentum? rng):
    c = full_conv(a)
    P = c.max()
    if P <=0: return a
    w = smooth_weights(c,beta)
    grad = _grad_from_weights(w,a)
    # scale grad to mean 1
    gmean = grad.mean()
    if gmean <=0: ...
    x = grad / (P + 1e-300) # O(1)
    xmean = x.mean()
    # To avoid large exponent, clip
    y = a * np.exp(-eta * (x/xmean - 1.0))
    y = np.clip(y, 0.0, None)
    s = y.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: return a
    return y/s
```
But subtracting mean in exponent: `x/xmean -1` has mean ~0, so sum of y maybe close. Normalize.

We can after optimization clip values below a threshold (e.g., 1e-12) to 0, renormalize, evaluate. But if too many zeros, score worsens; only if improves. We can test.

LP helper:
```
def lp_direction(a, B=100.0, use_dense=True):
    n = len(a)
    # normalize a sum1
    c = full_conv(a); M=c.max()
    # build A_ub
    if n <= 600:
       # dense toeplitz: first col a padded, first row [a0,0...]
       c_col = np.zeros(2*n-1); c_col[:n]=a
       r_row = np.zeros(n); r_row[0]=a[0]
       A = scipy.linalg.toeplitz(c_col, r_row)
       # But check: M[i,j] for i<j uses r_row[j-i]. That is zero except j=0 impossible; good.
       res = linprog(c_vec, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1,M), bounds=[(0,B)]*n, method='highs')
    else:
       # use sparse diags maybe
```

How to run LP iterations:
```
def lp_improve(a, budget_s, max_iters=20, t0=0.5, t_end=0.1):
    best=a, best_score=score(a)
    for it in range(max_iters):
       b=lp_direction(a)
       if b is None: break
       for t in [0.5,0.2,0.1,0.5?]: 
           cand=(1-t)*a+t*b; normalize; sc=score(cand)
           if sc<best_score: a=cand; best_score=sc
    return best
```

LP algorithm detailed:
Given normalized f (sum1). M=max(f*f). Solve LP maximizing Σ b with b≥0 and f*b ≤ M. Since scaling invariant, b will be small; rescale to sum1.
If f isn't far from uniform, LP direction may be identity/no improvement. Starting random/asymmetric needed. The update f ← (1-t)f + t b. The SOTA says nonincreasing M. We can use t=0.5 or adaptive.

Can we solve LP faster via **column generation / active constraints** for large n? Since A is Toeplitz lower triangular. We can maybe use a simple iterative algorithm for LP because matrix is monotone? Let's examine primal LP:
Max sum b_j
s.t. for all k=0..L-1: Σ_{j=max(0,k-n+1)}^{min(n-1,k)} a_{k-j} b_j ≤ M.
All a positive. This is a packing LP with intervals (sliding). Usually maximizing sum with capacity constraints can be greedy from left to right if constraints are laminar/nested, but sliding windows not laminar. However if a nonnegative, constraints for increasing k shift right; not nested.

Could use **dual multiplicative weights**: Minimize M Σ y_k s.t. (a*y)_j ≥1. We can solve this via simple subgradient: initialize y uniform; constraints shortages v_j=(a*y)_j-1. Update y_k *= exp(η * something)? This is like reducing sum y while satisfying A^T y≥1. Maybe easier: We want a probability y over coefficients (sum Y =Σy) such that for all j, (a*y)_j ≥ Y? Wait scaling dual: minimize Y=M Σ y_k s.t. (a*y)_j ≥1. If scale y by 1/Y, constraints (a*q)_j ≥1/Y where q sum1. The minimal Y is 1 / max_{q simplex} min_j (a*q)_j? Actually min over y≥0 MΣy s.t. A^T y≥1. Equivalent to M / max_{q:Σq=1} min_j (A^T q)_j? Let q=y/Σy, then A^T q ≥1/Σy. Max t such that A^T q ≥ t. Thus Y_star = M / t_star where t_star = max_{q prob} min_j (A^T q)_j. Primal dual strong. But how get b? Complementary slackness: b_j positive where A^T y=1. Hard.

But perhaps we can approximate b by solving for q that maximizes minimum coverage, then set b proportional to deficit? Not.

** CVXPY** could solve LP with sparse and be easier? But SciPy better.

LP update sequence:
```
def lp_iteration(a, time_per_lp=15.0):
    n=a.size
    c=full_conv(a); M=max
    A = ...
    cvec=-np.ones(n)
    bounds=[(0, B)]*n
    res=linprog(..., options={'time_limit': time_per_lp})
    if not res.success or res.x is None: return None
    b=res.x; b[b<0]=0
    s=b.sum()
    if s<=0: return None
    return b/s
```

Pseudo:
```
def optimize_length(n, seed, budget_s, use_lp=True):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # parametric scan
    candidates = generate_candidates(n, rng, count=200)
    best = min by peak_score
    best_score = peak_score(best)
    current = best.copy()
    no_improve=0
    beta_peak=100.0; eta=0.2
    lp_count=0
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       # occasionally LP
       if use_lp and n<=1024 and lp_count<max_lp and (should_lp):
          b=lp_direction(current)
          if b is not None:
             for t in ...: cand=...
             if improved: current=...; best=...
          lp_count +=1
       # gradient step with line search
       base_score=custom_peak_score(current)
       improved=False
       for eta_try in [eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25]:
           cand = multiplicative_step(current, beta_peak, eta_try)
           sc=custom_peak_score(cand)
           if sc < base_score - 1e-12:
              current=cand; base_score=sc; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
          # perturbation
          sigma = 0.05 + 0.05*min(1, no_improve/10?)
          current = add_noise(current, sigma, rng)
          # maybe keep if not much worse? no
       if base_score < best_score:
          best=current; best_score=base_score; no_improve=0
          # adapt eta increase? 
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve > 50:
             eta *=0.8; if eta<1e-3 reset restart? 
       # Occasionally introduce new random tilt if stagnating
       if no_improve > 200:
          current = generate_parametric(random)
          best still; no_improve=0
    return best,best_score
```

`multiplicative_step`:
```
def multiplicative_step(a, beta_peak, eta):
    c=full_conv(a); P=c.max()
    if P<=1e-300: return a
    z=beta_peak*(c/P) # since beta=beta_peak/P, beta*c = beta_peak*(c/P)
    # z max=beta_peak
    p=exp(z - beta_peak) / sum(exp(...))
    grad = correlation(p,a)
    # grad scale? Since p sum1, grad ~ average of a. a sum1, n length. `g_rel = grad / (P)`; mean maybe ~ (sum grad)/(nP) =? sum grad_j = Σ_k p_k Σ_j a_{k-j} = Σ_k p_k 1 =1. So mean grad =1/n. P~1/n, so mean g_rel ~1. Good.
```
This is stable. Clip z; p uniform if beta_peak small.

Log ratio: `u = grad / (P * n?)`? Wait `grad` sum=1. For a uniform, grad ~1/n, P~1/n, grad/P ~1. Good. For nonuniform, some positions may have grad/P >1 or <1. Exponent `-(eta)*(grad/P)/mean -?` Let's use `g = grad / P; mu = mean(g); logratio = -eta * (g/mu - 1)`. Sum of logratio weighted by? Not exactly zero, but normalize. If mu=0 skip. `eta` 0.1-0.5.

We need compute `grad` via w=p. Since p sum1, `grad.sum()` should be 1 exactly (sum over j,k p_k a_{k-j} =1). Good. `mu=1/n`. Can use `mean`.

Since a=1/n. So g_j = (1/n)*mass of p in interval [j,j+n-1]. grad/P = mass(interval) (because P=1/n). mu=1. For edges, interval misses some p mass (near opposite edge), so g<1. LP construction for dense:
```
def build_convolve_matrix_dense(a):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    col = np.zeros(L); col[:n]=a
    row = np.zeros(n); row[0]=a[0]
    A = scipy.linalg.toeplitz(col, row)
    return A
```
Let's verify with n=2 a=[a0,a1]. Toeplitz col [a0,a1,0], row [a0,0] => matrix [[a0,0],[a1,a0],[0,a1]]. Rows: k=0 [a0,0] correct; k=1 [a1,a0] correct; k=2 [0,a1] correct. Good.

For sparse, if scipy.linalg not available? We can implement with `scipy.sparse.diags` if imported. But dense enough for n<=1024.

Parametric generation:
```
def generate_initial_candidates(n, rng, count=100):
    out=[]
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    # uniform with noise
    for sig in [0.1,0.3,0.6,1.0]: out.append(normalize(np.exp(rng.normal(0,sig,n))))
    # exponential tilts
    for lam in [-5,-3,-2,-1,-0.5,0.5,1,2,3,5,8,12]:
       v=np.exp(lam*x); if max>0 and min finite: out.append(normalize(v))
    # power tilts
    for p in [0.2,0.5,1,2,4,8]:
       v=(x+1e-6)**p; out.append(normalize(v))
       v=(1-x+1e-6)**p
    # sine/cosine
    for alpha,beta in ... but ensure nonnegative: 1 + alpha*cos(pi x + phase) maybe alpha<1.
    out.append(normalize(1 + alpha*np.cos(2*np.pi*x + phase)))
    # random permutation? maybe not.
    return out
```
Evaluate each with `peak_score`, keep best few. For long n=2535, FFT okay.

`_safe_float` maybe.

`_normalize(a)`:
```
a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
a = np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
s=a.sum()
if s<=0 or not finite: return np.ones_like(a)/a.size
return a/s
```

`_fft_conv(a,b)` top-level:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float); b=np.asarray(b,float)
n=a.size; m=b.size; need=n+m-1
size=1
while size<need: size <<=1
fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size); fa*=np.fft.rfft(b,size)
out=np.fft.irfft(fa,size)[:need]
out[~np.isfinite(out)]=0.0
out[np.abs(out)<1e-30]=0.0
# For nonnegative inputs, small negative possible
out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
return out
```
Clipping all negatives to 0 may hide real negative? Inputs nonnegative, true convolution nonnegative, so okay. But due to FFT of nonnegative, negative roundoff only. Good.

`_peak_score(a)`:
```
s=a.sum()
if s<=0: return inf
c=_fft_conv(a)
return float(2*a.size*max(c)/(s*s))
```
For speed with sum1: `_peak_score_unit(a)` assumes sum1; use max(c)*2n.

`_weights_from_peak(c, method, beta_peak, top_frac, rng)`:
```
P=float(np.max(c))
L=c.size
if P<=0: return np.full(L,1/L)
if method <0.5: # smooth
   z=beta_peak*(c/P)
   z-=beta_peak
   # to avoid underflow exp; clip lower -745
   z=np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0)
   p=np.exp(z); s=p.sum(); if s<=0 uniform; else p/=s
else:
   # quantile
   if L<=1: ...
   # sort expensive if many iterations; use np.quantile O(L) okay. Or use threshold = P*(1-top_frac*?); We want topfrac coefficients. Use np.partition maybe.
   thresh = np.quantile(c, 1.0 - top_frac)
   mask=c >= thresh
   count=mask.sum()
   if count==0: mask = c==P? find max index
   p=np.zeros(L); p[mask]=1.0/count
return p
```
Quantile every iteration O(L) okay. Could use sorted? no.

`_gradient_weights(w,a)`:
```
wr = w[::-1]
conv = _fft_conv(wr, a)
L = w.size
idx = L - 1 - np.arange(a.size)
g = conv[idx]
return np.maximum(g,0.0)
```
Need ensure idx within conv length L+n-1; L-1 max to 0 okay.

`_multiplicative_step(a, beta_peak, eta, method, top_frac, log_clip=40)`:
```
c=_fft_conv(a); P=c.max()
if P<=0: return a
w=_weights_from_peak(c, method,beta_peak,top_frac)
g=_gradient_weights(w,a)
# sums maybe
gmax=g.max()
if gmax<=0: return a
g = g / (P + 1e-300)  # O(1)
mu = g.mean()
if mu<=0: return a
ratio = (g/mu - 1.0)
ratio = np.clip(ratio, -log_clip/eta? Actually logratio=-eta*ratio. Clip ratio to +/- 30/eta? Use logratio clip.
logr = -eta * ratio
logr = np.clip(logr, -50.0, 50.0)
y=a*np.exp(logr)
y = np.clip(y,0,None)
s=y.sum()
if s<=0: return a
return y/s
```
Note if a has zeros, multiplying zeros remains zero; could prevent recovery. Add tiny floor: before step, `a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)` perhaps. Or use `loga = log(a+eps)`. Simpler: `a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)` in step. But this can add tiny mass.

# polynomial tilt: 1 + a x + b x^2 nonnegative; sample a,b
for i in range(20):
    a=rng.uniform(-2,2); b=rng.uniform(-2,2); c=rng.uniform(0,2)
    v=c+a*x+b*x*x
    if min(v)<0 or max(v)<=0: continue

# trigonometric
for i in range(20):
    A=rng.uniform(0.1,0.9); B=rng.uniform(0.1,0.9); ph=rng.uniform(0,6.28); f=rng.uniform(1,5)

    # maybe add sine

       v += B*np.sin(2*np.pi*f*x)
    if min(v)<=0: continue
    outs.append(_normalize(v))
# random piecewise linear: random knots
... maybe.
return outs

LP:
```
def _lp_direction(a, time_limit=20.0):
   if not _LP_AVAILABLE: return None
   n=a.size
   if n > 1024: return None # or 2048 if time
   a=_normalize(a)
   c=_fft_conv(a); M=float(np.max(c))
   if M<=0: return None
   col=np.zeros(2*n-1); col[:n]=a
   row=np.zeros(n); row[0]=a[0]
   try: A=toeplitz(col,row)
   except Exception: return None
   cvec=-np.ones(n)
   B=1000.0
   bounds=[(0.0, B)]*n
   b_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M)
   try:
       res=linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'output_flag': 0, 'time_limit': time_limit})
   except Exception:
       return None
   x=res.x
   if x is None: return None
   x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return None
   x=np.clip(x,0,None)
   # feasibility check
   try:
      residual = A.dot(x)
      if np.max(residual) > M*(1+1e-5)+1e-8: 
          # maybe still use if slightly? skip
          pass
   except Exception: pass
   s=x.sum()
   if s<=1e-12: return None
   return x/s
```
Calling norm from scipy; if not imported, skip.

LP loop in optimizer:
```
def _lp_improve(current, best, best_score, max_iters, deadline):
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
       tlp = 20.0 if n>=768 else 15.0
       b=_lp_direction(current, tlp)
       if b is None: break? or continue
       for t in (0.5,0.3,0.2,0.15,0.6,0.75,1.0):
           cand=(1-t)*current + t*b
           cand=_normalize(cand)
           sc=_peak_score(cand)
           if sc < best_score:
              best=cand; best_score=sc; current=cand
           elif sc < current_score:
              current=cand
       # if no improvement after a few LP, stop
    return current,best,best_score
```
But A.dot for feasibility each time expensive; optional. We can skip or only if res not success.

Upsampling helper:
```
def _upsample(a, n_target):
    m=a.size
    if m==n_target: return a.copy()
    x0=np.linspace(0,1,m); x1=np.linspace(0,1,n_target)
    v=np.interp(x1,x0,a)
    return _normalize(np.maximum(v,0.0))
```
For non-smooth sequences, interpolation okay.

Downsample:
```
def _downsample(a, n_target):
    if a.size == n_target: return a
    x0=np.linspace(0,1,a.size); x1=np.linspace(0,1,n_target); v=np.interp(x1,x0,a); normalize
```

Pseudo optimize_sequence:
```
def optimize_sequence(n, seed, budget_s, use_lp=False, max_lp=10, lp_time=20.0):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s
    # generate candidates
    cands=[]
    # maybe include height_sequence_1 downsample if n
    start = _make_initial_solution(n, rng)
    current=start; best=start; best_score=_peak_score(best)
    current_score=best_score
    no_improve=0
    beta_peak=80.0; eta=0.25; top_frac=0.1; mode=0
    lp_done=0
    lp_improve_count=0
    step=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       # Every 20 steps maybe LP at start and if stuck
       if use_lp and n<=512 and lp_done<max_lp and (step<5 or no_improve>30):
          b=_lp_direction(current, min(lp_time, max(2, deadline-time.monotonic())))
          if b is not None:
             old=current_score
             for t in [...]:
                cand=_normalize((1-t)*current + t*b)
                sc=_peak_score(cand)
                if sc<current_score: current=cand; current_score=sc
                if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best=cand.copy(); improved
             if current_score < old: lp_improve_count+=1
          lp_done +=1
          if lp_done>=max_lp: use_lp=False? 
       # gradient line search
       base=current_score
       improved=False
       for eta_try in (eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25, eta*2?):
          cand=_multiplicative_step(current, beta_peak, eta_try, mode, top_frac)
          sc=_peak_score(cand)
          if sc < base - 1e-14:
             current=cand; current_score=sc; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
          # try alternative weights mode? maybe if mode smooth, try hard
          eta_try=eta
          for mode_try in (1,0):
             cand=_multiplicative_step(current,beta_peak,eta_try,mode_try,top_frac)
             sc=_peak_score(cand)
             if sc<base-1e-14: current...; improved=True; break
       if current_score < best_score:
          best=current.copy(); best_score=current_score; no_improve=0
          if eta < 0.5: eta = min(0.5, eta*1.02)
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve % 20 ==0:
             # perturb
             sigma = 0.03 + min(0.2, no_improve*0.001)
             pert = _add_noise(current, sigma, rng)
             sc=_peak_score(pert)
             # accept if not too worse? maybe if better
             if sc < current_score:
                current=pert; current_score=sc
             else:
                # maybe keep but reset no_improve to allow escape? Could set current = current * mix
                cand = _normalize(0.7*current + 0.3*pert)
                sc2=_peak_score(cand)
                if sc2 < current_score:
                   current=cand; current_score=sc2
                else:
                   # do nothing but maybe reduce eta
                   eta=max(0.02, eta*0.8)
          if no_improve > 1000:
             # restart from new random parametric but keep best
             newc, newscore = _fast_best_parametric(n, rng)
             current=newc; current_score=newscore
             no_improve=0
       step+=1
       # adapt top_frac maybe decreases? top_frac = max(0.02, 0.2 - 0.0001*step)
    return best,best_score
```

Detailed helper implementations:

1. `_as_array(a)`:
```
def _as_array(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim !=1:
        arr = arr.reshape(-1)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return arr
```

2. `_normalize(arr, fallback_n=None)`:
```
def _normalize(arr):
    arr = _as_array(arr)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        n = arr.size if arr.size>0 else 256
        arr = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        return arr / n
    return arr / s
```
If arr empty, returns uniform length 256? But caller provides.

3. `_uniform(n)`: return np.ones(n,dtype)/n (sum1). Use exact uniform.

4. `_fft_conv(a,b=None)`:
Need if b None. Use `np.fft.rfft` each time. For same a, can square. For gradient, different sequences. To improve, no caching because lengths vary.
```
def _fft_conv(a,b=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if b is None:
        need = 2*a.size -1
        size=1
        while size<need: size <<=1
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
        out = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)[:need]
    else:
        b=np.asarray(b,dtype=np.float64)
        need=a.size+b.size-1
        size=1
        while size<need: size <<=1
        fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,size)
        out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,size)[:need]
    out = np.asarray(out, dtype=np.float64)
    out[~np.isfinite(out)] = 0.0
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    return out
```
For b length maybe w and a. Good.

5. `_peak_score(arr)`:
```
def _peak_score(arr):
    a=_as_array(arr)
    if a.size==0: return inf
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: inf
    c=_fft_conv(a)
    mp=float(np.max(c))
    return 2.0*float(a.size)*mp/(s*s)
```
For unit, use `_peak_score_unit(a)` where sum1; but `_peak_score` recomputes. In step we can call `_peak_score` on normalized arrays, sum1. The sum computation small.

6. `_make_weights(c, mode, beta_peak, top_frac)`:
Mode could be 0 smooth, 1 hard, 2 gauss? Use int.
```
def _make_weights(c, mode, beta_peak, top_frac):
    L=c.size
    if L==0: return np.array([])
    P=float(np.max(c))
    if P <= 0.0:
       return np.full(L, 1.0/L)
    if mode <= 0:
       z = beta_peak * (c / P)
       z = z - beta_peak
       z = np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0)
       p = np.exp(z)
       s = float(p.sum())
       if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
          return np.full(L,1.0/L)
       p = p/s
    else:
       # hard top frac; if frac small use quantile
       frac = min(0.95, max(0.001, float(top_frac)))
       if L <= 1:
          return np.array([1.0])
       # Use min threshold via partition to avoid full sort? np.partition
       thresh = float(np.partition(c, max(0, L - max(1,int(np.ceil(frac*L)))))[-max(1,int(np.ceil(frac*L)))]? Simpler quantile.
       thresh = float(np.quantile(c, 1.0 - frac))
       mask = c >= thresh
       cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
       if cnt <= 0:
          mask = c == float(np.max(c))
          cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
       if cnt <= 0:
          mask = np.zeros(L,bool); mask[int(np.argmax(c))]=True; cnt=1
       p = np.zeros(L, dtype=float); p[mask] = 1.0/float(cnt)
    return p
```
Using quantile every iteration; okay. For hard, threshold can include many if c has flat. Fine.

7. `_grad_from_weights(w,a)`:
```
def _grad_from_weights(w,a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    wr = w[::-1].copy()
    conv = _fft_conv(wr, a)
    L = w.size
    idx = L - 1 - np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    g = conv[idx]
    g = np.maximum(g,0.0)
    # Correct if sum not 1? not needed
    return g
```
Check if conv length at least L+n-1; yes.

8. `_multiplicative_step(a, beta_peak, eta, mode, top_frac)`:
```
def _multiplicative_step(a,beta_peak,eta,mode,top_frac):
    a=_as_array(a)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return a
    a=np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    a=a/float(a.sum())
    c=_fft_conv(a)
    P=float(np.max(c))
    if P <= 1e-300: return a
    w=_make_weights(c,mode,beta_peak,top_frac)
    g=_grad_from_weights(w,a)
    if g.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        return a
    gp = g / (P + 1e-300)
    mu = float(gp.mean())
    if mu <= 1e-300:
        return a
    rat = (gp / mu) - 1.0
    logr = -float(eta) * rat
    logr = np.clip(logr, -60.0, 60.0)
    y = a * np.exp(logr)
    y = np.maximum(y,0.0)
    s = float(y.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return a
    return y/s
```
Could update log scale instead of a to avoid underflow: use `loga = np.log(a+1e-300) + logr - mean(loga?)` softmax. But a values may become tiny/exponential; we clip logr. Normalize. Fine.

9. `_add_noise(a, sigma, rng)`:
```
def _add_noise(a,sigma,rng):
    a=_as_array(a)
    if a.size==0: return _uniform(256)
    t=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size)
    gamma = float(rng.normal(0.0,0.3))
    logf = rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), size=a.size) + gamma*t
    y = a * np.exp(logf)
    return _normalize(y)
```
No closures. Good.

12. `_downsample(a, n)`:
```
def _downsample(a,n):
    a=_as_array(a)
    if a.size==0: return _uniform(n)
    if a.size==n: return a.copy()
    x0=np.linspace(0,1,a.size, dtype=float)
    x1=np.linspace(0,1,n, dtype=float)
    v=np.interp(x1,x0,a)
    return _normalize(v)
```
Upsample same.

Let's write `_optimize_sequence` more concretely:
```
def _optimize_sequence(n, seed, budget_s, use_lp, initial):
    n=int(max(8,n))
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max(0.0,budget_s)
    # initial
    best_arr=None; best_score=inf
    current=None
    if initial is not None:
       arr=_normalize(initial); sc=_peak_score(arr); current=arr; current_score=sc; best_arr=arr.copy(); best_score=sc
    else:
       arr, sc = _fast_best_parametric(n, rng, 80)
       current=arr; current_score=sc; best_arr=arr.copy(); best_score=sc
    # scan again small if initial given
    if initial is not None:
       arr, sc = _fast_best_parametric(n, rng, 20)
       if sc < current_score: current=arr; current_score=sc
       if sc < best_score: best_arr=arr.copy(); best_score=sc
    # global
    g=_tracked_global_sequence()
    if g is not None:
       gv=_downsample(g,n); gsc=_peak_score(gv)
       if gsc < current_score: current=gv; current_score=gsc
       if gsc < best_score: best_arr=gv.copy(); best_score=gsc
    step=0; no_improve=0; eta=0.25; mode=0; beta_peak=70.0; top_frac=0.15
    lp_allowed = bool(use_lp and _LP_AVAILABLE and n <= 512)
    lp_done=0; max_lp=12; lp_stalls=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # LP when due
       if lp_allowed and lp_done < max_lp and (step < 2 or no_improve >= 20):
           rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
           if rem < 3.0: break
           tlp = min(20.0, max(2.0, rem * 0.15))
           b = _lp_direction(current, tlp)
           lp_done += 1
           if b is None:
               lp_allowed = False
           else:
               old = current_score
               improved_lp=False
               for t in (0.1, 0.05, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5, 0.75):
                   cand = (1.0 - t) * current + t * b
                   cand = _normalize(cand)
                   sc = _peak_score(cand)
                   if sc < current_score:
                       current = cand; current_score = sc; improved_lp=True
                   if sc < best_score:
                       best_score = sc; best_arr = cand.copy()
               if improved_lp:
                   no_improve = 0; lp_stalls = 0
                   eta = min(0.5, eta + 0.05)
               else:
                   lp_stalls += 1
                   if lp_stalls >= 3:
                       lp_allowed = False
       # gradient
       base = current_score
       improved=False
       # choose beta_peak schedule: start 70, maybe reduce if no improve? 
       for mode_try in (mode, 1-mode):
           for eta_frac in (1.0, 0.5, 2.0, 0.25):
               eta_try = eta * eta_frac
               cand = _multiplicative_step(current, beta_peak, eta_try, mode_try, top_frac)
               sc = _peak_score(cand)
               if sc < base - 1e-13:
                   current = cand; current_score = sc; improved=True; mode = mode_try; # keep mode
                   break
           if improved: break
       if improved:
           if current_score < best_score:
                best_score = current_score; best_arr = current.copy()
                no_improve = 0
                # slow anneal: top_frac maybe decrease, eta stable
                if top_frac > 0.05: top_frac *= 0.999
           elif no_improve >= 1: no_improve = 1
           else: no_improve = 1
       else:
           no_improve += 1
           if no_improve == 10:
               mode = 1 - mode
           elif no_improve == 30:
               eta = max(0.05, eta * 0.7)
           elif no_improve > 50 and no_improve % 25 == 0:
                sigma = 0.03 + min(0.25, no_improve * 0.002)
                cand = _add_noise(current, sigma, rng)
                sc = _peak_score(cand)
                if sc < current_score:
                    current = cand; current_score = sc
                else:
                    # optionally mix with best_arr if best much better
                    mix = 0.5 * current + 0.5 * best_arr
                    cand = _normalize(mix)
                    sc2 = _peak_score(cand)
                    if sc2 < current_score:
                        current = cand; current_score = sc2
           if no_improve > 800:
                arr, sc = _fast_best_parametric(n, rng, 20)
                if sc < current_score:
                    current = arr; current_score = sc
                else:
                    # keep current but reset to avoid infinite? 
                    pass
                no_improve = 0
                eta = 0.25
                mode = 0
       # schedule beta: increase slightly with step to focus
       if step < 2000 and beta_peak < 400:
           beta_peak += 0.05
       step += 1
    # final local zero small?
    if best_arr is not None:
        a0 = best_arr / max(float(best_arr.sum()),1e-300)
        for frac in (1e-7,1e-6,1e-5):
            v = _zero_small(a0, frac)
            sc = _peak_score(v)
            if sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc; best_arr = v
    return best_arr, best_score
```

def _fast_best_parametric(n, rng, count=80):
    n = max(2, int(n))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    best = None
    best_score = float('inf')
    # helper inline update: v = _normalize(v); sc = _peak_score(v); if sc < best_score: ...
    v = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64); v = _normalize(v); sc = _peak_score(v); ...
    for sig in (0.05,0.1,0.18,0.3,0.5,0.8,1.2,1.8):
       v = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sig,n)); ...
    for lam in (-8,-5,-3,-2,-1.5,-1,-0.7,-0.4,0.4,0.7,1,1.5,2,3,5,8,12):
       v = np.exp(lam * x); ...
    for p in (0.1,0.25,0.5,1,2,4,8):
       v = (x + 1e-8)**p; ...
       v = (1.0 - x + 1e-8)**p; ...
    # random polynomial/trig until count reached? count param maybe not used if loops fixed. We can track generated.
    trials=0
    while trials < count:
       a = rng.uniform(-2,2); b=rng.uniform(-2,2); c=rng.uniform(0.2,2)
       v = c + a*x + b*x*x
       if v.min() > 0:
          ...
       trials +=1
    trials2=0
    while trials2 < count//2:
       A=rng.uniform(0.05,0.95); B=rng.uniform(0.0,0.8); phase=rng.uniform(0,2*pi); freq=rng.choice([1,2,3,4])
       v=1 + A*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x + phase)
       if B>0: v = v + B*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*x)
       if v.min()>0:
          ...
       trials2+=1
    if best is None: best = _uniform(n); best_score = 2.0
    return _normalize(best), float(best_score)

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

The implementation combines:
- FFT-based evaluation of convolution peaks.
- Asymmetric parametric/random initialisation.
- Smooth-max and top-coefficient multiplicative projected descent.
- Optional active-set LP directions (SOTA LP approach) for moderate lengths.
- Exact evaluator verification before returning the best found sequence.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

_TWO_PI = 2.0 * np.pi

_LP_AVAILABLE = False
_toeplitz = None
_linprog = None
try:
    from scipy.linalg import toeplitz as _toeplitz
    from scipy.optimize import linprog as _linprog
    _LP_AVAILABLE = True
except Exception:
    _LP_AVAILABLE = False


def _as_array(x):
    """Convert to a finite, non-negative float array."""
    a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        a = np.zeros(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a


def _normalize(a):
    """Return a non-negative finite array with sum one."""
    a = _as_array(a)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0.0:
        n = max(2, int(a.size))
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(n)
    return a / s


def _uniform(n):
    """Uniform sum-one sequence of length n."""
    n = max(2, int(n))
    return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)


def _fft_conv(a, b=None):
    """
    Full linear convolution using FFT.
    Inputs are non-negative; small negative FFT roundoff is clipped to zero.
    """
    a = _as_array(a)
    if b is None:
        b = a
    else:
        b = _as_array(b)

    need = int(a.size) + int(b.size) - 1
    if need <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, size)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, size)[:need]

    out = np.asarray(out, dtype=np.float64)
    out[~np.isfinite(out)] = 0.0
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    return out


def _peak_score(a):
    """Fast score using FFT. Equivalent to evaluate_sequence for valid inputs."""
    a = _as_array(a)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    c = _fft_conv(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if (not np.isfinite(mx)) or mx < 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * a.size * mx / (s * s))


def _update_best(v, best, best_score):
    """Return (sequence, score) if v improves best, else unchanged."""
    if v is None:
        return best, best_score
    arr = np.asarray(v)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return best, best_score
    x = _normalize(v)
    sc = _peak_score(x)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        return x, float(sc)
    return best, best_score


def _global_sequence():
    """Fetch height_sequence_1 if present in the execution namespace."""
    try:
        seq = height_sequence_1
    except NameError:
        return None
    a = _as_array(seq)
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    s = float(a.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return a


def _downsample(a, n):
    """Piecewise-linear downsample/upsample to length n."""
    a = _as_array(a)
    n = max(2, int(n))
    if a.size == 0:
        return _uniform(n)
    if a.size == n:
        return a.copy()
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.interp(x1, x0, a)
    return _normalize(y)


def _upsample(a, n):
    return _downsample(a, n)


def _make_weights(c, mode, beta_peak, top_frac):
    """
    Convert convolution coefficients c into non-negative weights for a descent step.

    mode 0: smooth log-sum-exp weights.
    mode 1: hard/uniform weights on the top-scored coefficients.
    """
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    L = int(c.size)
    if L == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    P = float(np.max(c))
    if (not np.isfinite(P)) or P <= 0.0:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / float(L), dtype=np.float64)

    if mode <= 0:
        z = float(beta_peak) * (c / P) - float(beta_peak)
        z = np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0)
        p = np.exp(z)
        s = float(p.sum())
        if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 0.0:
            return np.full(L, 1.0 / float(L), dtype=np.float64)
        p = p / s
        return p

    frac = float(top_frac)
    if not np.isfinite(frac):
        frac = 0.15
    frac = min(0.95, max(0.005, frac))

    thresh = float(np.quantile(c, 1.0 - frac))
    mask = c >= thresh
    cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
    if cnt <= 0:
        mask = np.zeros(L, dtype=bool)
        mask[int(np.argmax(c))] = True
        cnt = 1

    p = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    p[mask] = 1.0 / float(cnt)
    return p


def _grad_from_weights(w, a):
    """
    For weights w over convolution coefficients, compute the subgradient
    vector whose j-th component is sum_k w_k a_{k-j}.
    """
    a = _as_array(a)
    w = _as_array(w)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0 or w.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    wr = w[::-1].copy()
    conv = _fft_conv(wr, a)
    L = int(w.size)
    idx = L - 1 - np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    g = conv[idx]
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    g[~np.isfinite(g)] = 0.0
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    return g


def _multiplicative_step(a, beta_peak, eta, mode, top_frac):
    """
    One projected multiplicative step.

    The sequence is kept positive and normalised.  The step reduces positions
    that are paired repeatedly by currently active convolution coefficients.
    """
    a = _as_array(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a

    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    a = _normalize(a)

    c = _fft_conv(a)
    P = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if (not np.isfinite(P)) or P <= 0.0:
        return a

    w = _make_weights(c, mode, beta_peak, top_frac)
    g = _grad_from_weights(w, a)
    if g.size != n or (not np.all(np.isfinite(g))):
        return a

    gp = g / (P + 1e-300)
    mu = float(gp.mean())
    if (not np.isfinite(mu)) or mu <= 1e-300:
        return a

    ratio = (gp / mu) - 1.0
    logr = -float(eta) * ratio
    logr = np.clip(logr, -60.0, 60.0)

    y = a * np.exp(logr)
    y = np.maximum(y, 0.0)
    s = float(y.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 1e-300:
        return a
    return y / s


def _add_noise(a, sigma, rng):
    """Small asymmetric noise/mixing to escape local minima."""
    a = _as_array(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return _uniform(256)

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    gamma = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.35))
    logf = rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), size=n) + gamma * t
    y = a * np.exp(logf)
    return _normalize(y)


def _zero_small(a, frac):
    """Optionally zero the smallest mass fractions and renormalize."""
    a = _normalize(a)
    if a.size == 0 or frac <= 0.0:
        return a
    q = float(np.quantile(a, float(frac)))
    v = np.where(a > q, a, 0.0)
    return _normalize(v)


def _lp_direction(a, time_limit=20.0):
    """
    SOTA LP direction:
        max 1'b
        s.t. b >= 0, 0 <= b <= B,
             (a * b)_k <= max(a * a) for all k.
    Only used for moderate lengths where the dense LP is cheap.
    """
    if not _LP_AVAILABLE:
        return None

    a = _normalize(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 16 or n > 512:
        return None

    c = _fft_conv(a)
    M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if (not np.isfinite(M)) or M <= 0.0:
        return None

    L = 2 * n - 1
    col = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    col[:n] = a
    row = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    row[0] = a[0]

    try:
        A = _toeplitz(col, row)
    except Exception:
        return None

    b_bound = max(1000.0, 20.0 * float(n))
    c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.full(L, M, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, b_bound)] * n

    try:
        res = _linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={
                "presolve": True,
                "output_flag": 0,
                "time_limit": float(time_limit),
            },
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    x = res.x
    if x is None:
        return None

    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size != n or (not np.all(np.isfinite(x))):
        return None

    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, None)

    if not bool(res.success):
        try:
            viol = float(np.max(A.dot(x)))
            tol = M * 1e-6 + 1e-8
            if viol > M + tol:
                return None
        except Exception:
            return None

    s = float(x.sum())
    if (not np.isfinite(s)) or s <= 1e-14:
        return None
    return x / s


def _fast_best_parametric(n, rng, count=80):
    """
    Generate a bank of asymmetric initial sequences and return the best one
    under the fast FFT score.
    """
    n = max(2, int(n))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    best = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    best, best_score = _update_best(_uniform(n), best, best_score)

    for sig in (0.05, 0.1, 0.18, 0.30, 0.50, 0.80, 1.2, 1.8):
        v = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, size=n))
        best, best_score = _update_best(v, best, best_score)

    for lam in (-8.0, -5.0, -3.0, -2.0, -1.5, -1.0, -0.7, -0.4,
                0.4, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0, 8.0, 12.0):
        v = np.exp(lam * x)
        best, best_score = _update_best(v, best, best_score)

    for p in (0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0):
        v = (x + 1e-8) ** p
        best, best_score = _update_best(v, best, best_score)
        v = (1.0 - x + 1e-8) ** p
        best, best_score = _update_best(v, best, best_score)

    # Asymmetric beta-like shapes.
    exponents = (0.0, 0.25, 0.50, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0)
    for aa in exponents:
        for bb in exponents:
            v = (x + 1e-7) ** aa * (1.0 - x + 1e-7) ** bb
            if v.size and float(np.min(v)) > 1e-300:
                best, best_score = _update_best(v, best, best_score)

    # Random positive polynomials.
    trials = 0
    limit = max(0, int(count))
    while trials < limit:
        a1 = float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0))
        b1 = float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0))
        c0 = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 2.0))
        v = c0 + a1 * x + b1 * x * x
        if v.size and float(np.min(v)) > 1e-9:
            best, best_score = _update_best(v, best, best_score)
        trials += 1

    # Random trigonometric asymmetries.
    trials = 0
    limit = max(0, int(count) // 2)
    while trials < limit:
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.10, 0.95))
        amp2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.70))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, _TWO_PI))
        fr = int(rng.integers(1, 5))
        v = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(_TWO_PI * float(fr) * x + ph)
        if amp2 > 0.0:
            v = v + amp2 * np.sin(_TWO_PI * float(fr) * x)
        if v.size and float(np.min(v)) > 1e-9:
            best, best_score = _update_best(v, best, best_score)
        trials += 1

    if best is None:
        return _uniform(n), _peak_score(_uniform(n))
    return _normalize(best), float(best_score)


def _optimize_sequence(n, seed, budget_s, use_lp, initial=None):
    """
    Local optimisation for one length n.
    Combines optional LP moves with multiplicative descent.
    """
    n = max(16, int(n))
    budget_s = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + budget_s

    best_arr = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    current = None
    current_score = float("inf")

    if initial is not None:
        arr = _normalize(initial)
        sc = _peak_score(arr)
        current = arr
        current_score = sc
        best_arr = arr.copy()
        best_score = sc
    else:
        current, current_score = _fast_best_parametric(n, rng, 90)
        best_arr = current.copy()
        best_score = current_score

    # Additional scan helps refine the starting point when an initial is given.
    if initial is not None:
        alt, alt_score = _fast_best_parametric(n, rng, 25)
        if alt_score < current_score:
            current = alt
            current_score = alt_score
        if alt_score < best_score:
            best_arr = alt.copy()
            best_score = alt_score

    g = _global_sequence()
    if g is not None:
        gv = _downsample(g, n)
        gsc = _peak_score(gv)
        if np.isfinite(gsc):
            if gsc < current_score:
                current = gv
                current_score = gsc
            if gsc < best_score:
                best_arr = gv.copy()
                best_score = gsc

    step = 0
    no_improve = 0
    eta = 0.25
    mode = 0
    beta_peak = 70.0
    top_frac = 0.18

    lp_allowed = bool(use_lp and _LP_AVAILABLE and n <= 512)
    lp_done = 0
    lp_stalls = 0
    max_lp = 14

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # Optional LP move.
        if lp_allowed and lp_done < max_lp and (step < 3 or no_improve >= 18):
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 3.0:
                break
            tlp = min(22.0, max(2.0, rem * 0.12))
            b = _lp_direction(current, tlp)
            lp_done += 1
            if b is None:
                lp_allowed = False
            else:
                old = current_score
                improved_lp = False
                for t in (0.10, 0.05, 0.20, 0.30, 0.50, 0.75):
                    cand = (1.0 - t) * current + t * b
                    cand = _normalize(cand)
                    sc = _peak_score(cand)
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        if sc < current_score:
                            current = cand
                            current_score = sc
                            improved_lp = True
                        if sc < best_score:
                            best_score = sc
                            best_arr = cand.copy()
                if improved_lp:
                    no_improve = 0
                    lp_stalls = 0
                    eta = min(0.60, eta + 0.04)
                else:
                    lp_stalls += 1
                    if lp_stalls >= 3:
                        lp_allowed = False

        # Multiplicative descent with a small line search.
        base = current_score
        improved = False

        for eta_frac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25):
            cand = _multiplicative_step(current, beta_peak, eta * eta_frac, mode, top_frac)
            sc = _peak_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < base - 1e-13:
                current = cand
                current_score = sc
                improved = True
                break

        # Occasionally try the alternate weighting mode.
        if not improved and (no_improve in (8, 22) or step % 40 == 0):
            cand = _multiplicative_step(current, beta_peak, eta, 1 - mode, top_frac)
            sc = _peak_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < base - 1e-13:
                current = cand
                current_score = sc
                improved = True
                mode = 1 - mode

        if improved:
            if current_score < best_score:
                best_score = current_score
                best_arr = current.copy()
                no_improve = 0
                if top_frac > 0.05:
                    top_frac *= 0.999
            else:
                no_improve = min(no_improve + 1, 10000)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve == 10:
                mode = 1 - mode
            if no_improve == 30:
                eta = max(0.04, eta * 0.70)
            if no_improve > 40 and no_improve % 25 == 0:
                sigma = 0.03 + min(0.30, no_improve * 0.002)
                cand = _add_noise(current, sigma, rng)
                sc = _peak_score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current_score:
                    current = cand
                    current_score = sc
                else:
                    mixed = 0.55 * current + 0.45 * best_arr
                    cand2 = _normalize(mixed)
                    sc2 = _peak_score(cand2)
                    if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < current_score:
                        current = cand2
                        current_score = sc2

            if no_improve > 700:
                arr, sc = _fast_best_parametric(n, rng, 20)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current_score:
                    current = arr
                    current_score = sc
                no_improve = 0
                eta = 0.25
                mode = 0

        if step < 2500 and beta_peak < 450.0:
            beta_peak += 0.04

        step += 1

    # Tiny support cleanup at the end.
    if best_arr is not None:
        a0 = best_arr
        for frac in (1e-8, 1e-7, 1e-6, 1e-5):
            v = _zero_small(a0, frac)
            sc = _peak_score(v)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_arr = v

    if best_arr is None:
        best_arr = _uniform(n)
        best_score = _peak_score(best_arr)

    return best_arr, float(best_score)


def _array_to_list(a):
    a = _normalize(a)
    if a.size == 0:
        a = _uniform(2535)
    return [float(v) for v in a]


def _exact_score(seq):
    """Use the authoritative evaluator safely."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except NameError:
        return float("inf")
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return float("inf")
    return val


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point.  Searches with a time limit and returns the best valid
    non-negative list found.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    total = 1800.0
    if budget_s is not None:
        try:
            total = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
        except Exception:
            total = 1800.0

    reserve = 70.0
    deadline = start + max(0.1, total - reserve)

    records = []

    fallback = _uniform(2535)
    records.append((fallback, _peak_score(fallback)))

    g = _global_sequence()
    if g is not None:
        gv = _normalize(g)
        records.append((gv, _peak_score(gv)))

    best_custom_arr = fallback
    best_custom_score = min(2.0, _peak_score(best_custom_arr))
    for arr, sc in records:
        if sc < best_custom_score:
            best_custom_score = sc
            best_custom_arr = arr

    work_budget = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())

    # (length, fraction of work budget, use_lp)
    plans = (
        (256, 0.08, True),
        (512, 0.27, True),
        (1024, 0.30, False),
        (2535, 0.30, False),
    )

    plan_index = 0
    for n, frac, use_lp in plans:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 30.0:
            break
        alloc = work_budget * frac
        alloc = min(alloc, max(0.0, rem - 5.0))
        if alloc <= 5.0:
            break

        initial = None
        if best_custom_arr is not None:
            if best_custom_arr.size == n:
                initial = best_custom_arr.copy()
            else:
                initial = _upsample(best_custom_arr, n)

        try:
            arr, sc = _optimize_sequence(
                n=n,
                seed=int(seed) + plan_index,
                budget_s=alloc,
                use_lp=use_lp,
                initial=initial,
            )
        except Exception:
            arr, sc = best_custom_arr, best_custom_score

        arr = _normalize(arr)
        sc = _peak_score(arr)
        records.append((arr, sc))

        if sc < best_custom_score:
            best_custom_score = sc
            best_custom_arr = arr

        print(
            "plan n={} alloc={:.1f}s best_fft={:.10f}".format(
                n,
                alloc,
                sc if np.isfinite(sc) else float("inf"),
                flush=True,
            )
        )
        plan_index += 1

    # If meaningful time remains, polish the currently best length.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 150.0 and best_custom_arr is not None:
        polish_n = int(best_custom_arr.size)
        polish_budget = min(700.0, max(0.0, rem - 60.0))
        try:
            arr, sc = _optimize_sequence(
                n=polish_n,
                seed=int(seed) + 1000,
                budget_s=polish_budget,
                use_lp=(_LP_AVAILABLE and polish_n <= 512),
                initial=best_custom_arr,
            )
            arr = _normalize(arr)
            sc = _peak_score(arr)
            records.append((arr, sc))
            if sc < best_custom_score:
                best_custom_score = sc
                best_custom_arr = arr
            print(
                "polish n={} budget={:.1f}s best_fft={:.10f}".format(
                    polish_n,
                    polish_budget,
                    sc if np.isfinite(sc) else float("inf"),
                    flush=True,
                )
            )
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Exact evaluator final check.
    best_seq = None
    best_exact = float("inf")

    for arr, _ in records:
        seq = _array_to_list(arr)
        v = _exact_score(seq)
        if np.isfinite(v) and v < best_exact:
            best_exact = v
            best_seq = seq
        else:
            continue

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _array_to_list(_uniform(2535))
        best_exact = _exact_score(best_seq)

    if not np.isfinite(best_exact):
        best_seq = [1.0] * 2535
        best_exact = _exact_score(best_seq)

    print("final exact_score={:.10f}".format(best_exact if np.isfinite(best_exact) else float("inf")))
    return best_seq
```
